The decline in water resources will be primarily shown in the agricultural and animal-related sector, while hydroelectric production will be affected. Temperature changes will also affect tourism. As winter tourism declines, summer tourism potential will rise due to the expansion of the Mediterranean climate. For these reasons, all necessary measures must be taken to ensure the country's minimal impact on climate change. At the beginning, there are problems with storage of fresh water. In order to benefit more from fresh water, studies must be conducted to minimize evaporation. To do the work that will be reduced to a minimum, you have to create underground basins and underground dams. That's how much I care. In the last 15 years, we've made significant investments in infrastructure, physical infrastructure. Now it's time for us to make better use of this physical infrastructure and make more progress in productive areas. When I say the new growth story, or the new development story, I mean that we're going to go through the middle income trap first, and we're talking about promoting our country to the highest human development of high-income countries. So just because the laws of the state speak of the legal state and its principles is not enough for the state of law. Therefore, actions aimed at implementing the rule of law should not be empty, without ground; actions aimed at practice should be carried out directly and positively. The most important factor that undermines the legal state in Turkey is that those who use the executive or legislative force use these forces as a means of enjoyment or political self-interest. The independence of the judiciary, which operates as a control and brake mechanism in securing the legal state, has unfortunately been ignored for the sake of arbitrary issues. In the past 17 years, the government has repeatedly intervened in the judiciary, making it completely impossible for the judiciary to take any step outside of the government's will. Without that understanding, any rights issue in Turkey is not possible to be opened with clarity. The Turkish judiciary has been criticised for its lack of independence and neutrality over the years. So, how do you strengthen the state of law? The basic rule of law is that the state is bound by the law, not by the arbitrary decisions, but by the rule of law in its activities. The important thing is that the law is the law that appeals to the general, which is not personal and is abstract. Another important point is that the language of the law should be clear and clear, not cause different interpretations. At the same time, these rules of law should not be changed frequently and very easily, so it should be created an environment where individuals can predict what kind of legal treatment they will face in the face of a situation and act accordingly. This principle faces various violations in our country. In our country, laws are constantly changed, and the process of making laws has even been transformed into an experiment area or a test board. Is it possible that in a situation where even we lawyers have a hard time following the laws that have been changed, citizens are aware of these laws? Dear lawmakers, the neutrality of the state is fundamentally that the state does not adopt any ideological view. This is another important principle of the state of law. It is far from a law state, which is not in accordance with this principle, which is formalizing an ideology. Such a state can force its inhabitants into one kind of life, ignore those who do not embrace its own ideology, and expose them to abuse. The law begins to serve the state, or rather the ideology adopted by the state. What we've been saying since the beginning is that the greatest obstacle to the state of law is the joy that goes through the roof in every area. Equality in front of the law is also associated with the principle of neutrality of the state. In order to talk about a legal state, that state has to be equal to its citizens. Although a formal ideological view has not been adopted in our country, practices have shown the opposite, especially in the last 17 years. If the development of the state of law is required in Turkey, these practices must end, and the state must respect everyone's opinion, not only impose its own ideology and one kind of life on its people but work for its well-being. This requires that the public give more care when choosing those who rule them.
